### Slide 56

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A multi-panel line chart shares a common wave-annotated timeline, letting four indicators be compared against the same cycle.

**Text**

Are we entering / a new wave of / the crypto price-innovation cycle — or a false start? / Global crypto market cap¹ / $4T / Wave 1 / Wave 2 / Wave 3 / Wave 4 / Wave 5? / $3T / $2T / $1T / $0 / Crypto search interest² / 100 / 75 / 50 / 25 / 0 / Active crypto developers³ / 80K / 60K / 40K / 20K / 0 / Crypto startup funding rounds⁴ / 800 / 600 / 400 / 200 / 0 / 2011 / 2012 / 2013 / 2014 / 2015 / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / 2022 / 2023 / 2024 / 56 / 1/ Source: CoinGecko as of 9/30/2024. / 2/ Search interest across crypto terms (cryptocurrency, crypto, bitcoin, ethereum, blockchain, web3) | Source: Google Trends + GTAB as of 9/30/2024. / 3/ Number of unique GitHub accounts that committed to, forked, or starred a public crypto repository | Source: GitHub + Electric Capital Ecosystems as of 9/30/2024. / 4/ Source: PitchBook as of 9/30/2024. / ©2024 Andreessen Horowitz. | All rights reserved worldwide.

**Layout**

Two columns on a white slide: a left column of about 0 to 30 percent of the width holds a large bold title in a gradient panel, and a right column from about 36 to 97 percent holds four stacked charts sharing one 2011 to 2024 time axis, with a numbered footnote block along the bottom of the right column. A thin vertical gradient strip divides the two columns at about 29.5 percent of the width, the title is top-left aligned starting about 6.8 percent from the top, the page number sits bottom-right at about 97.8 percent x and 96.2 percent y, and the logo and copyright sit bottom-left.

**Visuals**

Four stacked time-series charts with a shared horizontal axis of years 2011 to 2024 and a left-aligned chart title above each. Chart 1, Global crypto market cap¹: line chart with a light orange line (about #F9B45A) on a y-axis of $0 to $4T with ticks $0, $1T, $2T, $3T and $4T; the line is near zero until about 2017, reaches about $0.9T around 2018, falls to about $0.2T to $0.3T through 2019 and 2020, climbs to about $2.5T in early 2021, peaks at about $3T in late 2021, falls to about $1T in 2023, and returns to about $3T in 2024 (values about, no labels printed on the points). Chart 2, Crypto search interest²: vertical bar chart in green (light green #A4D17A at early years through dark green #5FAE3A for 2021 to 2022) on a y-axis of 0 to 100 with ticks 0, 25, 50, 75 and 100 (no unit); bars are near 0 until 2017, peak at about 60 to 63 in 2018, reach about 85 in early 2021 and about 75 in 2022, and sit around 25 to 30 in 2023 and 2024 (about). Chart 3, Active crypto developers³: vertical bar chart with bars shading from dark green #0B6B4F (2021 to 2023) to grey-green #7FA89A (2024) on a y-axis of 0 to 80K with ticks 0, 20K, 40K, 60K and 80K (developers, no unit printed beyond K); bars rise from near 0 in 2011 to about 15K in 2017, about 40K in 2018, about 35K in 2020, and peak at about 75K in 2022 with values about 60K to 70K through 2023 and about 60K in 2024 (about). Chart 4, Crypto startup funding rounds⁴: vertical bar chart with bars in dark teal-blue #1B5E7A for 2018 to 2023 and grey-blue #7A9DAA for some later bars on a y-axis of 0 to 800 with ticks 0, 200, 400, 600 and 800 (rounds, no unit printed); bars are near 0 until 2013, reach about 150 to 300 in 2018 to 2019, about 450 in early 2021, and the tallest bar is about 710 in 2022 (about), with most bars between about 150 and 450 in 2021 to 2024. Highlighted points: the Wave 5? label is highlighted with an orange box. Vertical grey bands mark the periods of Wave 1 (2011 to about 2013), Wave 3 (about 2017 to 2021) and Wave 5 (about 2023 to 2024); thin vertical grey dividers mark wave boundaries in the top chart. No legend. Chart title superscripts 1 to 4 refer to the footnotes.

**Design**

Background: Left panel: diagonal gradient from mint #C8F5DC through cream #FAF8D2 to peach #FDE4DC; right area: solid white #FFFFFF. Left panel background is a soft diagonal gradient from mint #C8F5DC at the top-left through pale cream-yellow #FAF8D2 at the bottom-left to peach #FDE4DC at the bottom-right; the right area is plain white #FFFFFF. Title text is dark navy #12335A in bold humanist sans-serif (Noto Sans Bold, about 4.9 percent of slide height per line, roughly 6.9 percent for capital letters). Chart titles are bold navy #12335A about 2.1 percent of slide height. Tick and axis labels are Noto Sans Medium about 1.6 percent in grey #595959. Wave labels are Noto Sans Medium about 1.5 percent in navy #12335A with Wave 5? in bold. Footnotes are Noto Sans Regular about 1.4 percent in grey #595959. Copyright is Noto Sans Medium about 1.2 percent in light grey #D9D9D9. Page number is Noto Sans Medium about 1.5 percent in grey #999999. Accent highlight is orange #FBB965 behind 'new wave' and behind 'Wave 5?'. Vertical divider strip is a gradient from green #3DDC84 at top through grey-olive to coral #FF8A8A at bottom. Shaded bands are #EBEBEB at 36 percent opacity with no outlines. Charts have thin light grey horizontal gridlines and no borders.

### Slide 57

**Text**

Some of our thoughts for 2024 and beyond...
The price-innovation cycle will continue to drive the crypto market. When prices go up, people get interested, developers build, and new products push the industry forward.
Crypto is becoming accessible to institutional investors. While spot Bitcoin and Ethereum ETPs are trading today, it will take time for the major distributors to activate. Eventually, crypto could become part of a diversified portfolio.
Blockchains can counterbalance AI’s tendency to consolidate power on the internet. We expect to see significant development at the intersection of these technologies.
Crypto has become a hot political topic. The U.S. elections may generate short-term noise, but over a longer timeframe we expect key legislation to pass — resulting in much-needed regulatory clarity for the industry.
Blockchain infrastructure is maturing. Nearly all of Ethereum's activity has moved to layer 2, with Coinbase's Base L2 leading the way. The result has been drastically reduced fees for users that unlock new applications.
Stablecoins are emerging as crypto's first "killer app.” As fees come down and more people are onboarded with crypto wallets, we could reach a tipping point where strong network effects kick in. Regulation will play an important role in determining winners in this category.
Crypto is increasingly global. With prominent political figures now weighing in, many nation states will seek to quickly develop a crypto strategy.
L2s aren’t the only way to scale blockchains. Other high-throughput blockchains are on a promising path. In addition, ZK-coprocessors present a whole new programming paradigm for scaling computation.
The potential for bigger ideas — decentralized social networks, a new consumer internet, the metaverse — may take longer to realize, but interesting early experiments are gaining traction.
Some of the above are a16z investments. For a full list see a16z.com/investment-list. This content should not be considered investment advice.
57
©2024 Andreessen Horowitz. | All rights reserved worldwide.

**Layout**

Title sits top-left in a full-width header band about 21% of the slide high, with a thin gradient rule beneath it. Below, three equal columns of rounded outline boxes (about 31% wide each, left margin about 2%, right margin about 2%) hold the body text, with the first column's boxes and the third column's boxes staggered in height; the footer row (logo and copyright at bottom left, footnote centred-left, slide number at bottom right) sits below the boxes at about 93% to 98% of the slide height.

**Visuals**

Thin horizontal gradient rule spanning full width at about 21.2% of slide height, running from green on the left to coral/pink on the right. A pale full-slide gradient wash in the header area fades from mint green at the left to pale pink at the right and to white toward the bottom of the header. Small a16z crypto logo wordmark in light grey at bottom left. Ten amber highlight bars sit behind bold phrases in the body text. No charts, photographs or tables.

**Design**

Background: White #FFFFFF with a mint-to-pale-pink gradient wash across the top header area. White background with a mint-to-pink gradient header wash. Title text navy #12335A, Noto Sans Bold, sans-serif, about 4.9% of slide height (cap height about 3.5%). Body text navy #12335A, Noto Sans Regular, about 2.35% of slide height, bold phrases in Noto Sans Bold. Highlight accent amber #FBB965 behind bold phrases. Gradient rule green #4ADE80 to coral #FF8A80. Boxes are white with thin slate outlines (#7F8FA6, about 0.75pt) and small corner radii of about 8px, no shadows. Footnote grey #595959, copyright light grey #D9D9D9, page number grey #999999.

58

**Layout**

Full-bleed slide with a pastel gradient background. The header sits top-left: a small subtitle above a large bold title, with a thin gradient rule beneath spanning almost the full width. A 2x2 grid of website screenshot cards with bold captions fills the left-centre, and a tall dark rounded panel on the right holds the podcast, newsletter and social links. The footer logo and copyright sit bottom-left and the page number bottom-right, all with left margin about 2 to 6 percent.

**Visuals**

Four website screenshot thumbnails in a 2x2 grid, each in a white rounded card with a soft drop shadow. (1) Top-left, Builder Energy Dashboard: pale yellow-green top bar with a small a16z crypto wordmark and a State of Crypto pill; a hero band with the large black outlined title BUILDER ENERGY on a yellow-to-pink gradient; below it text 'A resource based on data from thousands of crypto projects we've tracked.'; a 'Builder interest by blockchain' section with a bubble chart of blockchains (Optimism 6.7%, Avalanche 4.2%, Polygon POS 7.9%, BNB Chain 1.0%, Other Blockchain 4.5%) in red, orange, purple and blue circles, and a horizontal bar list on the right (Ethereum 20.8%, Solana 11.2%, Base) under 'Top 10 chains'. (2) Top-right, State of Crypto Index: off-white page with a black stencil-style headline STATE OF CRYPTO INDEX, two pale pink and green stat boxes, a 'state of crypto index' line chart with y-axis 1,000 to 5,000 and a light blue area line rising sharply at the right, an 'About this index' sidebar and a dark 'download report' button. (3) Bottom-left, Read Write Own: pale blue-to-peach gradient page with a New York Times bestseller badge, a 3D book cover titled Read Write Own, Building the Next Era of the Internet, by Chris Dixon, a sunset-and-sea illustration on the cover, the title and deck on the right, and 'available now' with four retailer buttons (amazon, barnes & noble, audible, kindle). (4) Bottom-right, Blog: white page with a green highlighted headline 'we back bold entrepreneurs building the next internet', a line 'a16z crypto is a venture capital fund that invests in crypto and web3 startups', and a list with three entries: Threshold ECDSA (Abhi Shelat), Exponent-VRFs and their applications (Yehuda Lindell), The past, present, and future of threshold Schnorr signatures (Chelsea Komlo), each tagged RESEARCH. Each screenshot has a dark rounded 'visit site' button in monospace white text overlaid at its lower-middle edge. In the right panel: a podcast banner image with the white text WEB3 and an a16z crypto logo on a deep magenta #841159 field with yellow-green zigzag stripes in the top-left and bottom-right corners; a green newsletter card; and five white monochrome icons in a column: X logo, LinkedIn 'in' square, YouTube play button, GitHub octocat mark and the Farcaster arch logo. Footer: a faint light-grey a16z crypto wordmark logo bottom-left. No photographs beyond the screenshots.

**Design**

Background is a soft pastel mesh gradient running from mint green at top-left through pale yellow at bottom-left and blush pink at centre to lilac and purple at the right edge, framed by a thin white inner border with stepped corners. Titles are navy #12335A, bold Noto Sans. Subtitle is #12335A medium weight. Title is about 4.9% of slide height and body and card captions about 2.5%. Panel is near-black #1F2328 with white regular Noto Sans text about 2.5% of slide height. Newsletter card #52D891 with black text. Social labels are white Noto Sans Mono regular, about 2.2%. Accent rule is a horizontal gradient bar from yellow through peach and pink to violet #7B2CBF. Cards have white backgrounds, rounded corners of roughly 1.5% of slide width, and soft grey drop shadows. Panel corners are rounded about 2% of width.
